| a custom `io` reader / writer saw only row text | public `io.ListAwareReader` (`RowList(col int, dst *io.ListCell) error`), `io.ListCell` and `io.ListState` let your reader feed list-sourced vectors with the built-ins' parity (without it a declaration reports `no_columns`); `RowList`'s error is a cell the source failed to READ — it aborts the import (dim probe included) exactly as a `ReadRows` failure does, a `*errors.CodedError` keeping its own code and gaining `vector` / `column` / `row` details, while a readable cell the vector cannot hold still goes through `ListCell.State`; public `io.ListAwareWriter` (`SetListColumns`, called before `WriteHeader`) receives each vector as ONE column whose cell is a reused `[]float64` (nil when null), where any other writer gets the vector expanded in place into `<field>_<label>` scalar columns (`<field>_1..N` unlabelled). Contract: `docs/src/internals/adding-io-format.md` (Native list cells) | added | implement the interface if your format has a native list type (return nil from `RowList` unless the read itself failed); otherwise do nothing and receive expanded columns | U29 |
| export typed columns by position | Excel, Arrow and Parquet export type each column from an explicit per-output-column plan. A projected (`Includes`) or `_label`-augmented export of any cohort used to type column `i` from the schema's `i`-th field: every row failed in Arrow / Parquet and Excel wrote raw or mis-formatted cells. Both now work and keep native types; a label REPLACE on a non-decimal numeric field exports as text in Arrow / Parquet. `io.PredictReport.Columns` lists the output column names; a vector expansion that equals another column's name is `PULSE_EXPORT_COLUMN_COLLISION`. A writer outside the library gets the same plan through the new public `io.ColumnAwareWriter` (`SetExportColumns(cols []io.ExportColumn)`, called once before `WriteHeader`, after `SetPulseSchema` and before `SetListColumns`; never on a `CohortWriter`, under `ConvertJob` or by `Predict`): `io.ExportColumn{Name, Kind, Source, Element, Field}` with `io.ExportColumnKind` (`ExportColumnField`, `ExportColumnLabel`, `ExportColumnElement`, `ExportColumnList`), and `io.ColumnFields(cols, schema, header)` resolves one typing field per header column (nil = text), falling back to the schema field of the same NAME without a plan | behaviour change (bug fix) + added | re-run any projected / augmented Arrow, Parquet or Excel export you worked around; a typed custom writer implements `io.ColumnAwareWriter` and types columns with `io.ColumnFields` (never by position) | U29 |
| `ConvertJob`, shards and `pulse widen` / `dedup` knew no vectors | `ConvertJob` refuses a schema that resolves a vector field (`PULSE_IMPORT_VECTOR_INVALID`, `reason: not_declared`: a convert is a text pass). Shard cohesion checks a vector's dim strictly (`PULSE_SHARD_SCHEMA_MISMATCH`, `details.dimension: vector_dim`); labels, kind and scale range tolerate divergence with `PULSE_SHARD_DESCRIPTION_DIVERGENCE` (`details.dimension` `vector_labels` / `vector_kind` / `vector_scale`). A vector may be a parent-group member or an elided constant; widen and dedup carry it unchanged | added | import a vector cohort with `Import` (or the builder), not `Convert` | U29 |
| synth knew scalar, categorical and set fields only | `Synth`, `synth from-schema`, `synth from-profile`, `profile create` and augment carry a `vec_f32` / `vec_f64` field. A spec declares it with `synth.FieldSpec.Vector` (`synth.VectorSpec{Dim, Kind, ScaleMin, ScaleMax, Labels, NaNRate, Elements []VectorElementSpec, Correlations []VectorCorrelationSpec}`; `FieldSpec.Distribution` is the default element family — normal, uniform, lognormal, exponential, bernoulli, discrete or mixture — and `kind` / scale range bound every element). A profile captures `synth.FieldProfile.Vector` (`synth.VectorProfile`: per-element `VectorElementProfile` marginals + NaN rate, pairwise-complete `VectorCorrelationStat` pairs, correlation abandoned above 64 elements with a warning); the fidelity report gains `FidelityReport.VectorFields` (`synth.VectorFieldFidelity` / `VectorElementFidelity`). A vector in a correlation, pair or model, or written by a rule `set` / `set_expr`, is refused | added | declare `vector` on a vec field in a hand-written spec; a vector-free spec, profile and report are byte-identical | U29 |
| `pulse_recommend` bound only scalar columns | a vector slot is drafted WHOLE as `"vector": "<field>"` (kind-aware) when the cohort has a native vector, and a vector is never bound into a scalar slot; `TEST_HOTELLING_T2` `split_by` binds only 2-level categoricals (a 4-level draft failed at runtime) | behaviour change (recommend drafts) | re-run recommend; stored drafts are unaffected | U29 |
| error code list stopped at the U28 codes | new codes: `PULSE_IMPORT_VECTOR_INVALID`, `PULSE_VECTOR_FIELD_NOT_SCALAR`, `PULSE_VECTOR_NAME_COLLISION` (`details.native`), `PULSE_EXPORT_COLUMN_COLLISION`; reused with new details: `PULSE_SHARD_SCHEMA_MISMATCH`, `PULSE_SHARD_DESCRIPTION_DIVERGENCE`, `PULSE_JOIN_TYPE_MISMATCH`, `PULSE_SPSS_SIDECAR_INVALID` (a vector cohort cannot take the SPSS sidecar path; `--ignore-sidecar` exports one numeric variable per element) | added | branch on the code, not the message | U29 |

Not shipped, by decision: `vec_u8` (stretch, not scheduled).

## Changes from U30 (matrix extensions and hardening)

Complete for U30. No release tag was cut by the unit; it ships in the next alpha (`v1.0.0-alpha.13`). Contract: `.claude/reference/matrix-and-vectors.md` (index → slices), `.claude/reference/architecture.md` (Extension surface), `.claude/reference/response-components.md` (Undefined figures on the wire). `format_version` stays `"1.1"` (every wire change is additive or a figure moving from a number to `null`).

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| an embedder could not add a `MAT_*` operator (`MAT_` was outside the extension naming policy) | `pulse.Extensions.MatrixOps []pulse.MatrixOpRegistration` (`Name` `MAT_<NAMESPACE>_<NAME>`, `Factory extend.MatrixFactory`, `Params`, `OutputKeys`, `ComponentSchema`, `Purpose`, `PValues`, `WeightAware`, `Streamable`, `Accepts`, `DependsOn`, `FieldInputs`) over the new `extend.MatrixOperator` (`Finalize(*extend.MatrixInput) (extend.MatrixOutput, error)`), `extend.MatrixInput` (the slot's `*linalg.CoMoment`, members, labels, params, `Rows`, `Square` / `Rect` builders) and `extend.MatrixRows`. Run-probed at `pulse.New`; manifest `extensions.matrix_ops` (`descriptor.MatrixMeta`). The engine owns `missing` / `max_drop_share` and strips them before `Finalize`; a registration declaring either is refused `PULSE_EXTENSION_PARAM_INVALID` (`reason: reserved_matrix_param`). `PValues` names the auxiliary p-value key the multiplicity fold adjusts. New code `PULSE_EXTENSION_MATRIX_OUTPUT_MISMATCH` (output keys differ from the declared ones) | added | register through `Extensions.MatrixOps`; results are bitwise worker-invariant like the built-ins' | U30 |
| `linalg.CoMoment` exposed no listwise state | `(*CoMoment).ListwiseState() (linalg.ListwiseState, error)`, `linalg.NewCoMomentFromListwise(ListwiseState)`, `(*CoMoment).PairM2(i, j)`; `ListwiseState{N, NMass, NWeightInvalid, W, W2, Mean, M2 *Sym}` | added | rebuild an accumulator from a saved state | U30 |
| `ComposedResponse.Fits` was `map[string]*types.RegressionResult` | `map[string]*types.FitResult`: `FitResult` embeds `*RegressionResult` (nil on a vector fit) and adds `Vector *types.VectorFitResult` (`operator`, `labels`, `members`, `basis`, `n`, `n_weight_invalid`, `sum_weights`, `n_star`, `mean`, `covariance`); `types.FitKind` (`FitKindRegression` / `FitKindVector`). The wire is unchanged for a regression fit; a vector fit is `{"vector": {...}}`. `descriptor.FitSummary` `type` / `target` / `predictors` became `omitempty` and gained `Vector *descriptor.VectorFitSummary` | replaced (Go type) | field reads through the embedding still compile; a variable typed `*RegressionResult` takes `fits[name].RegressionResult`; branch on `Vector != nil` | U30 |
| a compose `fits` entry took exactly one regression | a fit may also be a vector battery (one `MAT_*` or fitted attribute); fitted attributes (`ATTR_MAHALANOBIS`, `ATTR_PC_SCORE`, `ATTR_FACTOR_SCORE`) and every co-moment `MAT_*` read it through `params.fit` (a fit-bound matrix folds no row and refuses `groups`, `missing`, `max_drop_share`) | added | move a repeated `fit_on` refit into one compose fit | U30 |
| populations did not exist | a vector fit's `save` writes a `.pulse-population.json`; `Request.Populations []types.PopulationRef{Name, Path}` (root alias on `pulse.Request`) loads one by name; `pulse.Extensions.Populations map[string]pulse.Population{File []byte}`, `pulse.Options.PopulationsDir` and `PULSE_POPULATIONS_DIR` register them at `pulse.New` (manifest `extensions.populations`, `descriptor.PopulationMeta`). Codes reuse `PULSE_MODEL_*` (details `population`); new `PULSE_MODEL_KIND_MISMATCH`; directory-vs-programmatic collisions are `PULSE_EXTENSION_DUPLICATE`. Gated by `capability:models` / `capability:model_save` | added | save once, load or register by name | U30 |
| comparison attributes compared against a literal or a registered `ReferenceVectors` profile | `params.reference` also takes `{centroid: {by, filter}}` (a weighted per-member mean folded in a pre-pass; a row whose centroid no row reached is null + `PULSE_VECTOR_REFERENCE_MISSING`), `{population: name}` and `{record: {keys}}` (one stored record, looked up through the cohort's sidecar index) | added | build the index first for `record` | U30 |
| `ATTR_PROFILE_SIMILARITY` was not weight-aware | weight class `ClassAware` (weighted centroid); manifest `weight_aware` / `weight_kinds` | behaviour change | a weighted request's centroid similarity moves | U30 |
| vector aggregators stopped at `AGG_VEC_MEAN` / `AGG_VEC_SUM` | `types.AGG_VEC_SD`: per-member sample SD (n − 1; frequency Σw − 1; probability Kish n_eff − 1), a member with N* ≤ 1 is null; components `element_mean`, `element_m2`, `element_sum_weights_sq` | added | none | U30 |
| an expanded element column (`<label>[<element>]`) was presentation only | a sort key, window field / order_by / partition_by, post-test input, every overlay host's scored value and a chain stage (as nullable `f64`) read it; a crosstab cell or `margin_aggregations` entry takes `params.element` (a member's display label); a return path takes a quoted segment `data."emb_mean[q3]"` (`\"` / `\\` escapes, `"x"*` glob). A bare return-path name starting with `"` is now refused | added; behaviour change (return paths) | quote the segment | U30 |
| a window treated a NaN cell as a number (tied with everything); a chain stage passed NaN `f64` through; a vector `fields` glob / bracket form shadowed an exact schema name | NaN is null for every window caller (sort, order_by, partition_by, RANK ties); a NaN `f64` chain cell is null in the next stage; an exact schema name beats glob / bracket syntax | behaviour change (bug fix) | none | U30 |
| an aggregator over a bucket with no value (or Σw = 0) answered `0` | `null` (NaN in Go) on every arm (cohort, group, crosstab cell and margin, auxiliary margin): `AGG_AVERAGE` (incl. weighted), `AGG_WEIGHTED_MEAN` (+ `weighted_mean`), `AGG_MIN` / `MAX` / `RANGE` (+ components), `AGG_VARIANCE` / `STDDEV` (+ `mean` / `variance` / `stddev`; `m2` stays 0), `AGG_MEDIAN` / `PERCENTILE`, `AGG_MODE`, `AGG_SKEWNESS` / `KURTOSIS` at n = 0, `AGG_ZSCORE` (all four components with no value), `AGG_SET_CARDINALITY_AVG`; decimal128 `"0.00"` → `null`. Unchanged: `AGG_COUNT`, `AGG_SUM`, `AGG_NULL_COUNT`, `AGG_DISTINCT_COUNT`, `AGG_MODE_COUNT`, `AGG_SET_CARDINALITY_SUM`, `AGG_DISTINCT_SUM`. Knock-ons: a post-test over such a column has a null statistic and p; sort / windows read it as null; series overlays treat it as a missing point | behaviour change | read `null` (NaN) as undefined, never as 0 | U30 (E4-S5) |
| `AGG_SKEWNESS` / `AGG_KURTOSIS` at one value or zero spread, `AGG_ZSCORE` at zero spread, `AGG_WELFORD` and the `weighted_variance` component at Σw ≤ 1 answered `0` | `null` (scalar and the named components; `AGG_WELFORD` at zero spread with n ≥ 2 stays 0) | behaviour change | as above | U30 (E4-S6) |
| an overlay over an undefined (NaN) host cell or value | the entry keeps its key with the `statistic` key ABSENT (was `"statistic": null`), no spurious `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_ZERO`, out of `summary.count`, on the request, Compose (INDEX / DELTA / PANEL_INDEX / T / Z_VS_REF, the MATRIX arm, T / Z / PROP_Z_CELL) and chain (INDEX / DELTA_VS_STAGE) hosts; crosstab-host SHARE / INDEX / ZSCORE_VS_MARGIN and FORMULA skip the cell; chi-square / STD_RESIDUAL read it as absent; FISHER_EXACT_CELL absent; PAIRWISE_PROP_Z / PROBIT_T pairs skip with EXTRACT_FAILED; PAIRWISE_WELCH_T / TWO_MEANS_Z skip a leg with NaN variance and n > 1 (`ORBIT_STATS_SKIP_VARIANCE_INVALID`; `PULSE_STATS_SKIP_*` since U44) | behaviour change | test for the key, not for `null` | U30 (E4-S2, E4-S5, E4-S6) |
| `ATTR_FORMULA` with an empty `expression` (text placed in `params.expression`) passed predict | predict refuses with the runtime's `PROCESSING_CONFIG` message (Predict, PredictCompose, PredictChain) | behaviour change (refusal) | put the expression in `expression` | U30 (E4-S5) |
| pairwise + probability weight `MAT_RELIABILITY` / `MAT_PCA` (`on: covariance`) used one slot-wide Kish factor | each covariance entry takes its own pair's n_eff / (n_eff − 1) (diagonal: the item's rows); `item_sd`, `alpha`, `alpha_if_deleted`, `item_total_r`, covariance eigenvalues / loadings move; an entry whose pair n_eff ≤ 1 is null. `MAT_PCA` / `MAT_FACTOR` Bartlett's N* (and MAT_FACTOR's chi-square family) = the smallest pair's own Kish n_eff; `PULSE_MATRIX_PAIRWISE_N_STAR` `n_star` moves. Listwise and pairwise unweighted / frequency unchanged | behaviour change | expect moved figures on pairwise probability-weighted batteries | U30 (E5-S2) |
| `OVERLAY_CORRESPONDENCE` layers carried 6 parts; `metric.Consumers()` returned 5 | parts `row_distance` / `column_distance` (chi-square profile distances) make 8; `metric.ConsumerCorrespondence`, `metric.Consumers()` returns 6; manifest `matrix.metrics[chi_square].consumers` = `["OVERLAY_CORRESPONDENCE"]`, overlay consumers listed only when the instance offers the kind | added | a test pinning the part or consumer count updates it | U30 (E5-S3) |
| rotations were `varimax`, `promax`, `oblimin` | `MAT_FACTOR` / `ATTR_FACTOR_SCORE` add `quartimax`, `entropy`, `geomin_orthogonal` (orthogonal) and `geomin` (oblique); `MAT_PCA` / `ATTR_PC_SCORE` take the orthogonal ones; `params.epsilon` (default 0.01) for the geomin pair. Oblique on PCA: `reason` `bad_params` → `rotation_oblique_unsupported`; the `params.rotation` error lists valid names; `ATTR_PC_SCORE` `PULSE_MATRIX_NOT_CONVERGED` gains `details.solver` / `iterations` / `max_iterations` / `tolerance`; the message says "iteration cap", not "sweep cap". Oblimin is bit-identical. `oblimax` is not offered (owner decision: degenerate under GPFoblq) | added; behaviour change (reason, message) | match on `rotation_oblique_unsupported` | U30 (E6) |
| the overlay catalog stopped at the U28 kinds | `types.OverlayKindSymmetry` (`OVERLAY_SYMMETRY`, McNemar / Bowker on a paired from × to crosstab, `params.correct`, weight- and multiplicity-aware, empty pairs dropped) and `types.OverlayKindRetention` (`OVERLAY_RETENTION`, per from-state stay / switch / churn, `params.absorbing`); `PULSE_OVERLAY_EXPECTED_LOW` / `_NONNEGATIVE_REQUIRED` / `_MATRIX_NOT_SQUARE` messages cover them, and `MATRIX_NOT_SQUARE`'s detail keys are corrected to `row_groupers` / `column_groupers` | added; behaviour change (detail keys) | read the corrected keys | U30 (E7-S1 / S2) |
| a crosstab overlay spec predict refused on `ref` / `scope` ran silently at runtime | refused at runtime with predict's code, message and details on every crosstab arm (fused, buffered, Compose slot): `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE {index, kind[, axis]}` / `PULSE_OVERLAY_SCOPE_UNSUPPORTED {index, kind, scope}`; predict also refuses `ref.prior` / `rolling_mean` / `yoy` / `matrix` on CHISQ_*, FISHER_EXACT_CELL and the five PAIRWISE kinds; order Ref → Scope → no-crosstab | behaviour change (refusal) | drop the foreign arm or scope | U30 (E7-S3) |
| SERIES-host and FACET overlay specs with an unsupported scope, a foreign or missing Ref arm, `Ref.Prior.Lag ≠ 0`, Level / Within ≠ 0 ran; a Compose spec with empty `targets` passed predict; a ProcessChain stage's `Request.Crosstab` was dropped | refused as predict on every host (grouped Process, Compose slot, chain stage, `ApplySeriesOverlays`, Facet); predict-compose refuses empty `targets` (`PULSE_OVERLAY_TARGET_UNKNOWN`, label messages "label not found" / "label is empty", details `{which, slot_label}`); a whole-chain `StageRef` with both `Index` and `Name` is refused; a chain stage carrying a crosstab is `PULSE_CHAIN_NOT_MERGEABLE` on PredictChain and ProcessChain; stage overlay faults refuse before any stage runs (`details.stage`) | behaviour change (refusal) | fix the spec; run a crosstab as its own request | U30 (E7-S4) |
| an overlay on an ungrouped host with no crosstab or matrices (or a grouped request with no aggregations) returned no layer and no error | refused on predict and every runtime arm with `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE` (or the kind's scope / Ref refusal first); an ungrouped `OVERLAY_FORMULA` is refused "requires a MATRIX host (Request.Crosstab); none present" | behaviour change (refusal) | add the host the kind reads | U30 (E7-S5) |
| predict's matrix memory estimate under-counted (pairwise 7 words a pair, a 4-word header); `MaxEstimatedMemory` ignored shard-archive memory and charged an anchor-opened shard only its own records | pairwise `40 + 72·p(p+1)/2`, listwise `40 + 8·(p + p(p+1)/2)` bytes; an archive is charged its byte length, one schema decode and, when `ShardWorkers` fans out, the per-shard partial buckets; an anchor-opened shard is charged the whole archive (predict reads `Options.ShardWorkers`; `PredictBytes` unchanged). Archive open and scan no longer decode shard dictionaries, so real peak heap drops | behaviour change (estimates) | a run near the cap may now refuse (`PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`) or pass where it did not; re-check `Limits.MaxEstimatedMemory` | U30 (E8-S1, S3–S5) |
| `PULSE_VECTOR_FIELD_NOT_SCALAR`'s message and aggregation fixup named only `AGG_VEC_MEAN` / `AGG_VEC_SUM` | they name the whole vector-aggregator set (`AGG_VEC_MEAN`, `AGG_VEC_SUM`, `AGG_VEC_SD`) | behaviour change (message) | none | U30 (E8-S2) |

## Changes from U35 (predict / runtime parity)

Ships in `v1.0.0-alpha.13` (with U30). `format_version` stays `"1.1"`.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| shard cohesion ignored per-field `Nullable`: a shard declaring nullable a field the archive does not was admitted whenever the null-bitmap width matched, and its nulls decoded as values under the canonical schema; a shard dropping a nullable field's flag could be added even when its bitmap (and so its stride) differed | `PULSE_SHARD_SCHEMA_MISMATCH` with `details.dimension: "nullable"` (`field`, `canonical_nullable`, `incoming_nullable`; `canonical_bitmap_bytes` / `incoming_bitmap_bytes` on a width refusal) from `AddShard`, `CreateShardArchive`, `shard verify`, the anchored `NewCohortBuilder` pre-check and the archive record reader. A non-nullable shard field under a nullable archive field is still admitted when both bitmaps have the same width and the field is no parent-group member. Nullability is never widened | behaviour change (refusal) | import every shard with the archive's nullability, or seed the archive with a shard that declares the field nullable | U35 (E1-S1) |
| export (`ExportJob`, every row-loop target) read a bit-packed `u4` / `packed_bool` field's whole on-wire byte, ignoring its stored `BitPosition` | export decodes through the stored `BitPosition` like Process, SPSS export and Lookup; a legacy cohort with a nonzero `BitPosition` exports the values it processes (current writers always store 0: byte-identical) | behaviour change (output) | none; re-export a legacy cohort if an earlier export was relied on | U35 (E1-S2) |
| `ATTR_VEC_DISTANCE` / `ATTR_PROFILE_SIMILARITY` `key_field` and centroid `by`, and `ATTR_CODE_IN`, keyed a `u64` field by its float64 echo: distinct values above 2^53 collided, and a value above MaxInt64 took an arch-dependent out-of-range conversion | a `u64` key is matched by its exact stored value; narrower key types and values ≤ 2^53 unchanged; `extend.Record` stays float64 | behaviour change (output) | none; figures on large-`u64` keys move to the correct ones | U35 (E1-S2) |
| `WIN_LAG` / `WIN_LEAD` / `WIN_RUNNING_SUM` / `WIN_RUNNING_AVG` / `WIN_MOVING_AVG` / `WIN_EWMA` / `WIN_PCT_CHANGE` / `WIN_DELTA` over a categorical / `set_*` / `decimal128` / `packed_bool` / `datetime` field, `FEAT_POLY` over a categorical / `set_*` / `packed_bool` / `datetime` field, and `REG_OLS` / `REG_GLM` / `REG_BAYES_LINEAR` with a categorical (or `set_*`) target or predictor ran and returned numbers computed over a dictionary index, a bitmask or raw bytes (predict already refused them); tier-1 numeric tests over a `set_*` `field` / `field2` returned an n = 0 refusal or, for `TEST_SHAPIRO_WILK`, a result | `PULSE_FIELD_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED` (new code; details `{type, slot, field, field_type, accepts_types}`) for the windows, `FEAT_POLY` and regressions, `PULSE_TEST_FIELD_NOT_NUMERIC` / `PULSE_TEST_FIELD2_NOT_NUMERIC` for the tests — from `Process` / Compose / chain / Materialize and from predict alike (one shared table, `internal/fieldaccept`). Predict now raises `PULSE_FIELD_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED` where it raised `PULSE_WINDOW_INVALID` / `SERVICE_VALIDATION` for these refusals, refuses tier-1 tests on `set_*` (it accepted them) and `TEST_Z_TWO_SAMPLE` on a categorical. The manifest's regression `accepts_types` gains `datetime` (always accepted) | behaviour change (refusal) | window / expand / regress a numeric field; derive one first (`FEAT_ONE_HOT`, `ATTR_SET_POPCOUNT`, `ATTR_FORMULA`); match refusals on the new code | U35 (E2-S1) |
| Predict accepted a request `Process` refuses `PROCESSING_CONFIG` on field type: a non-set column on a `*_SET_*` operator; `set_*` on `AGG_DISTINCT_COUNT` / `FREQUENCY` / `MODE` / `MODE_COUNT` / `WELFORD`, `ATTR_NORMALIZED` / `PERCENTILE` / `TSCORE` / `ZSCORE` / `ATTR_REG_*`, `GROUP_CATEGORY` / `QUANTILE` / `RANGE` / `ROUNDED`, `FILTER_INCLUDE` / `EXCLUDE` / `RANGE`; a non-temporal field on `GROUP_DATE_RANGES` / `FILTER_DATE_RANGES`; a non-`packed_bool` field on strict-mode `FILTER_TRUE` / `FILTER_FALSE`; `AGG_WELFORD` off its six plain types; a categorical on `FILTER_RANGE` / `ATTR_REG_*`; decimal128 on `AGG_FREQUENCY` / `MODE` / `MODE_COUNT` / `AGG_SET_*` (only a `PULSE_AGG_NOT_MEANINGFUL_FOR_DECIMAL` warning) | predict reports one `PROCESSING_CONFIG` error, the runtime's code (details `{type, slot, field, field_type, accepts_types}`), at every slot including crosstab axes / cell / margins and facet filterers; `Process` is unchanged. `AGG_MODE` / `AGG_MODE_COUNT` `accepts_types` drop `decimal128` | behaviour change (predict refusal) | treat `valid: false` as the runtime's refusal; pick an operator whose `accepts_types` lists the field's type | U35 (E2-S2) |

## Changes from U44 (strict input)

Ships in `v1.0.0-alpha.14`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`: an owner exception — U44 fixes wire shapes in place without a bump.

A standalone `Process` response has none | additive | read `responses[i].label` instead of counting positions | U44 (E3-S2) |

| `pulse api lookup -i X -r req.json` (and `api facet`) read the request file's cohort whenever it named one; `-i` only filled an empty `cohort` | `-i` REPLACES the file's `cohort` (filename and `data_dir`), as the flag's help says; absent `-i` the file's cohort still drives the run | behaviour change (CLI precedence) | drop `-i` to run the file's cohort | U44 (E3-S3) |
| `pulse cohort inspect` took a positional `PATH` only; a missing path was an uncoded usage string (no envelope under `--json`) | it also takes `-i` / `--input` (the flag wins over a positional); a missing path is a `CLI_INPUT` envelope, exit 1 | additive (flag) + behaviour change (coded refusal) | none | U44 (E3-S3) |
| `Process` / `ProcessStream` record rows (`Response.Data` of an ungrouped windowed run), on Compose, `pulse api process` (text, `--json`, `--stream` NDJSON) and MCP `pulse_process`, rendered a `date` cell as its epoch day (`"d": 20454`) and a `datetime` cell as epoch seconds; a `WIN_LAG` / `WIN_LEAD` copy of a date likewise | `date` is `"2026-01-01"`, `datetime` `"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z"` (the Go value a `string`), a lag / lead copy of a date too; no zone applies. Windows, `order_by` and `Request.Sort` still read the epoch number, so order is chronological and a `WIN_DELTA` / running sum over a date stays a day count. Derived-cohort materialization is unchanged | behaviour change (wire + Go type) | parse the literal as for sample / lookup | U44 (E3-S5) |

## Changes from U45 (binary and decimal)

Ships in the next `v1.0.0-alpha`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| an `ATTR_FORMULA` / `FILTER_EXPRESSION` expression (or a `total()` argument) that failed expr-lang's compile was `PROCESSING_RUNTIME` `compiling <site> expression: <src>`, the parser's reason reachable only through `errors.Unwrap` (never on the wire, the CLI or MCP) | the new code `PULSE_FORMULA_COMPILE_ERROR`, at build and on a deferred first-record compile alike: message `compiling <site> expression "<src>": <reason>`, `details.compile_error` (expr-lang's reason with its line:column) and `details.expression` (the source); the compile error stays the cause. New error code in `errors.allCodes`. A run-time evaluation failure stays `PROCESSING_RUNTIME` | behaviour change (code, message, details) | match `PULSE_FORMULA_COMPILE_ERROR` for a bad expression; read `details.compile_error` | U45 (E2-S2) |
| predict checked only that an `ATTR_FORMULA` had an expression; a bad expression surfaced at build, or — when it read a derived column (an earlier attribute's label, a feature output) — on the first record, so an empty cohort never reported it | predict (request, Compose slot, chain stage, facet) and the runtime's entry check type-compile every `ATTR_FORMULA`, `FILTER_EXPRESSION` and `total()` argument before any record: the same `PULSE_FORMULA_COMPILE_ERROR`, code, message and details, on both sides. A derived column types as a number; an embedder `ExprFunction` is declared untyped (its misuse still surfaces at evaluation) | behaviour change (refused earlier, also at predict) | run predict; fix the expression `details.compile_error` names | U45 (E2-S3) |
| a formula reading an earlier attribute that was null on the cohort's FIRST record failed the whole request with `unknown name <label>` | it compiles (the null derived column is declared a number) and the row reads it as `nil`, like any null input | fix | none | U45 (E2-S3) |
| `AGG_COUNT` / `AGG_DISTINCT_COUNT` over a `decimal128` field emitted a quoted decimal string (`"4"`; a `decimalAggResult` in Go rows); `AGG_NULL_COUNT` over one was refused `PULSE_AGG_NOT_MEANINGFUL_FOR_DECIMAL` by predict and the runtime | a JSON integer (`4`; a `float64` in Go rows, as over every other field type) in every shape (cohort, group, stream, crosstab cell and margins, auxiliary margin); `AGG_NULL_COUNT` reads the null bitmap and runs on both sides, and the decimal refusal's `details.decimal_aggregations` lists it. The empty decimal `AGG_SUM` stays `"0.00"` | behaviour change (wire type) | read the count as a number, not a string; drop any decimal `AGG_NULL_COUNT` workaround | U45 (E3-S1) |
| a `decimal128` field bound in an `ATTR_FORMULA` / `FILTER_EXPRESSION` / `total()` expression as an opaque mantissa: `+ - * /` and ordering against it failed to compile, `==` compared mantissas ignoring the field scales, and a formula whose result was the decimal was refused `PROCESSING_RUNTIME` (`unsupported type`) | the decimal binds with its field scale and its operators are exact — decimal × decimal or decimal × number (a number joins as its shortest spelling, `1.2` is 1.2): `+ -` at `max(s1, s2)`, `*` at `s1 + s2` (held to 38, banker's), `/` at `max(s1, s2, 4)` (banker's); comparisons exact across scales; `price ?? x` and `c ? price : 0` lift the other side to a decimal; `float(price)` is the f64 escape. The numeric built-ins take a decimal: `abs`, `max`, `min`, `sum`, `mean`, `median`, `sort`, `sortBy` and `in` exact (`mean` / an even `median` divide at `max(scale, 4)`), `ceil` / `floor` / `round` an integral decimal (`round` half-even, so `round(2.5)` is 2), `int` truncates (past the int range `PULSE_DECIMAL_OVERFLOW`); `sqrt`, `log`, `exp`, `pow` and the vector functions read the decimal's float64; `toJSON` writes the decimal at its scale. An `ATTR_FORMULA` decimal result becomes the nearest float64 (the column stays float64). A zero divisor is `PULSE_DECIMAL_DIVIDE_BY_ZERO` and a result past 38 digits `PULSE_DECIMAL_OVERFLOW` (evaluation errors, under their own codes). `%` (its reason names the `float()` escape), a string or a bool operand still fails at compile (`PULSE_FORMULA_COMPILE_ERROR`, predict too), also beneath a decimal operator (`price / "x" > 1`). An embedder `ExprFunction` still receives `encoding.Decimal128` | behaviour change (expressions that failed now run; `==` across scales now compares values) | drop any explicit-attribute downcast workaround; write `float(x)` where float64 semantics are wanted | U45 (E3-S2) |
| the formula environment had no calendar helpers: weekday, month and period-start logic over a `date` was hand-coded epoch-day arithmetic, and a `datetime` (epoch seconds) read as a plain number | new reserved built-ins `log1p`, `weekday` (ISO 1 = Monday … 7), `month_index` (`year*12 + month-1`), `date_trunc(unit, day)` (`"week"` Monday start / `"month"` / `"quarter"` / `"year"`) over epoch days, and `to_day(ts)` — a datetime's local epoch day in the request `time_zone`, else `Options.DefaultTimeZone`, else UTC, on every entry point (the `FilterToFile*` family has no request zone, so it reads the default, and `FilterToFileWithRequest` keys its dedup name on a non-UTC one); a null argument answers null. A bare `datetime` field as a day helper's day, or a literal unknown unit, is `PULSE_FORMULA_COMPILE_ERROR` at predict and run (reason names `to_day()`); a per-row unknown unit is `PROCESSING_CONFIG`. `log1p` joins the scalar maths an `OVERLAY_FORMULA` admits | additive (functions) | write `weekday(to_day(ts))` for an instant; `month_index(a) - month_index(b)` for month offsets | U45 (E4-S1) |
| an embedder `ExprFunctions` entry could be named `log1p`, `weekday`, `month_index`, `date_trunc` or `to_day` | those names are reserved: `PULSE_EXTENSION_NAME_RESERVED` at `pulse.New` | behaviour change (registration refused) | rename the function | U45 (E4-S1) |
| `TEST_PROP_Z`, `TEST_CHISQ` (rows or cols), `FEAT_TRAIN_TEST_SPLIT` `stratify`, the encoders and every `split_by` refused a `packed_bool` field (`PULSE_FIELD_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED`), so a binary flag needed a categorical twin | a `packed_bool` is a two-level category wherever one is accepted: its levels read as the labels `"0"` / `"1"` (`TEST_PROP_Z` `success: "1"`), never as missing; `TEST_CHISQ` orders labels by first appearance, as for any categorical. Predict, the runtime and the manifest `accepts_types` agree | behaviour change (refusals now run) | drop a categorical twin of a flag; a caller matching the old refusal for a `packed_bool` now gets a result | U45 (E1) |
| `pulse recommend` / `pulse explain` (and `pulse_recommend`) never bound a `packed_bool` to a level slot (draft choice, ranking, the `why` text) | a `packed_bool` binds level slots as a two-group field (the `why` text says "2 groups") | behaviour change (drafts, rankings and `why` prose differ for cohorts with flags) | none | U45 (E1-S2) |
| a recommended k-group test (`TEST_ANOVA_F`, `TEST_ANOVA_WELCH`, `TEST_KRUSKAL_WALLIS`, `TEST_BROWN_FORSYTHE`) could bind a two-level split; `TEST_TUKEY_HSD` could draft one | the bound draft prefers a split with three or more levels for the k-group tests, and `TEST_TUKEY_HSD` never drafts fewer than three | behaviour change (bound drafts) | none | U45 (E1-S2) |
| `FilterToFile*` predicates had no calendar helpers, and a zoned predicate hashed like an unzoned one | a predicate calling `to_day` reads `Options.DefaultTimeZone`, and the zone joins the predicate hash, so the output name changes for a zoned predicate (`FilterToFileRequest` has no `TimeZone` field of its own; filed as #467); a predicate without `to_day`, or under a UTC default, hashes byte-identically | additive | none — only predicates using the new `to_day` are affected | U45 (E4-S1) |
| an `OVERLAY_FORMULA` calling a vector built-in was refused with "a vector built-in OVERLAY_FORMULA does not accept"; `log1p` did not exist | the refusal reads "a vector or calendar built-in OVERLAY_FORMULA does not accept" (scalar maths only), and `log1p` joins the scalar subset | behaviour change (message) / additive | none | U45 (E4-S1) |

## Changes from U47 (survey tables)

Ships in `v1.0.0-alpha.16`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; every additive row is byte-identical for a request that does not use it.

Release-notes grouping (rows below, in order): **behaviour breaks that move numbers or outputs** — own margins and denominators count only records that reached a cell (#325, "% of those asked"); pairwise `n_source` reads the cell's base, unweighted as a record count (owner-confirmed so frequency expansion and unity parity hold) (#324); `p_source` resolution on normalised hosts and the unnormalised mean-cell refusal; `total()` under a weight (#398); labelled `.sav` code order and set-indicator labels; derived cohorts carry the SPSS sidecar; banner label scoping. **Refusal changes** — `PULSE_OVERLAY_PARAM_MISSING` for an explicit `cell_value_pct` on a normalised host and for a mean cell without `p_source`; `PROP_Z_VS_PRIOR` over `AGG_WELFORD`; `PULSE_LABEL_DUPLICATE_BINDING`; `.sav` `--labels` no longer refused. **Relaxed refusals** — no column grouper, optional `cell`, `AGG_WELFORD` over attribute fields. **Additive** — `cells` (multi-measure crosstab), `letters`, `banner` (+ section overlays), `*_VS_PRIOR` overlays, `Pulse.Rake` / `pulse rake` / `pulse_rake`, SPSS value labels, `LabelTable.Order`, `Pulse.PredictExport` / `pulse export predict --labels`. Owner decisions that set defaults: significance `letters` alpha unset follows the multiplicity-adjusted verdict else raw p < 0.05; `PULSE_CROSSTAB_EMPTY_COLUMNS` stays registered but unraised; only pairwise overlays run on a multi-measure crosstab (others refused); no params-key hiding for `letters` in feature profiles; a value-label binding beside an honoured SPSS sidecar is refused (`--ignore-sidecar` relabels); the `compare_groups` scoped-manifest size exception (0.151) is accepted. Known limits: weighted prior layers stamp no `summary.parameters` `sum_weights` / `n_eff`; a Welch pairwise over mixed measure cell types is refused whole; carried SPSS variable names skip `--sanitize-names`.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a crosstab's OWN margins (the cell aggregator's `row_margins` / `column_margins` / `grand_total`, `components.crosstab.row_margin_counts` / `row_margin_components` / `column_margin_*` / `grand_total_*`) and every normalize denominator (`normalize: row` / `column` / `total`, `normalize_level`, `normalize_within`) counted every filter-passing record whose key resolved on THAT axis alone (the grand margin: every filter-passing record), so a respondent whose `GROUP_SET_PER_ELEMENT` set was null or empty, whose other axis key was null, or whose key a grouper `include` excluded reached no cell yet sat in the opposite axis's margins, the grand total and the denominators — column % read "% of everyone"; a partial-depth (`normalize_level`) denominator also counted a record whose deeper axis positions were null | a record reaches a margin or a denominator only if it reached a CELL (both axis keys resolved in full), on the fused and buffered arms alike: column % is "% of those asked" and the normalised cells under a margin share its admitted base. Axis KEYS are unchanged (a key whose records all failed the other axis still appears, its margin absent), as are cells, cell counts and `included_records` / `excluded_records`. A null CELL FIELD still reached its cell and still counts as the margin's `n_null`; auxiliary `margin_aggregations` figures are unchanged. The fused arm no longer emits a grand total of `0` when no record was admitted (absent, as on buffered) | behaviour change (numbers) | none for crosstabs whose every record resolves both axes (byte-identical); otherwise expect smaller margins and larger normalised shares. To reproduce the old "% of everyone" base, run the opposite axis alone (plain `groups`) for the denominator | U47 (E1-S1, #325) |
| `OVERLAY_PAIRWISE_PROP_Z` / `OVERLAY_PAIRWISE_PROBIT_T` with `params.n_source` omitted read the CELL's sample size — on a weighted host its N* (`sum_weights` / Kish `n_eff`), on an unweighted host `cell_n_unweighted` — even when the cell value was a share of a normalise denominator, so a column percentage was tested against a fraction of its column (the brand-tracker 28.6% vs 6.8% tested at p = 0.18 instead of < 0.001); on a weighted host `row_margin_n` / `column_margin_n` were `PROCESSING_CONFIG` | the omitted `n_source` reads the cell's BASE, the denominator its share is of: column margin (`normalize: column`), row margin (`row`), grand total (`total`), the `normalize_level` / `normalize_within` denominator — admitted — as a record count on an unweighted host and as N* on a weighted one (frequency weights still equal the expanded rows); under `normalize: none` the cell is its own base and nothing moves. `row_margin_n` / `column_margin_n` stand on a weighted host and read the displayed margin's floor N*. Layer `summary.parameters.sum_weights` / `n_eff` now total the distinct bases read. On a Welford kind a margin `n_source` on a weighted host is now predict's inert-selector `PULSE_OVERLAY_PARAM_MISSING` (runtime ignores it), as on an unweighted host | behaviour change (numbers) + relaxed refusal | none for `normalize: none` hosts (byte-identical); on a normalised host expect smaller p-values. To reproduce the old cell reading set `n_source: "cell_n_unweighted"` (unweighted hosts only) | U47 (E1-S2, #324) |
| an `ATTR_FORMULA` calling `total(expr)` summed the argument UNWEIGHTED whatever weight was in force (`Request.Weight`, a slot `weight` or `Options.DefaultWeight` was reported skipped) | the formula is frequency-only: under a `frequency` weight in force on its slot `total(expr)` is Σw·expr, the expanded-row total (an invalid / zero-weight row adds nothing); under a `probability` weight predict and runtime refuse it `PULSE_WEIGHT_UNSUPPORTED` (`details.function: "total"`, `details.reason`). A formula without `total()` still reads no weight | behaviour change (numbers) + new refusal | none for requests with no weight in force (byte-identical). To keep the unweighted total, set `"weight": null` on the attribute slot; under a probability weight either opt the slot out the same way or switch to a frequency weight | U47 (E1-S3, #398) |
| `OVERLAY_PAIRWISE_PROP_Z` / `OVERLAY_PAIRWISE_PROBIT_T` with `params.p_source` omitted read every cell as a 0..100 percentage (`cell_value_pct`, ÷100) even on a crosstab whose `normalize` is `row` / `column` / `total`, where each cell is already a 0..1 share — so every pair was scored on a proportion 100× too small, silently; an explicit `cell_value_pct` there did the same | on a normalised host the omitted `p_source` reads the share as is (`cell_value`); an explicit `cell_value_pct` on a normalised host is refused `PULSE_OVERLAY_PARAM_MISSING` (`details {kind, param: "p_source", p_source, normalize}`) at predict and runtime alike. Under `normalize: none` the omitted default stays `cell_value_pct` | behaviour change (numbers) + new refusal | none for `normalize: none` hosts or requests that already set `p_source: "cell_value"` (byte-identical). Drop an explicit `cell_value_pct` on a normalised host (or set `cell_value`) | U47 (E1-S3) |
| a crosstab with no column grouper (`columns` absent, `null` or `[]`) was refused `PULSE_CROSSTAB_EMPTY_COLUMNS` at predict and runtime; `types.CrosstabSpec.Columns` marshalled as `"columns": null` when nil, and the payload schema / MCP-bound schema listed `columns` as required | the column axis is optional: no column grouper yields ONE implicit column keyed `types.CrosstabTotalColumnKey` (`["Total"]`, `column_header {fields: ["_total"], types: ["TOTAL"]}`) holding every record whose row key resolved — on both arms, with margins, normalize and overlays over the one column (long shape: rows carry only the row fields). `PULSE_CROSSTAB_EMPTY_COLUMNS` is no longer raised (the code stays for lookups); `Columns` is `omitempty` and no longer `required`; a column-axis `normalize_level` (under `normalize: column`) or `normalize_within` (under `normalize: row`) on a no-column crosstab is `_OUT_OF_RANGE` (`axis_len: 0`) on predict as on runtime. New: `types.CrosstabTotalColumnKey` / `CrosstabTotalColumnField` / `CrosstabTotalColumnType`, `(*CrosstabSpec).HasImplicitTotalColumn`, `(*CrosstabSpec).ColumnHeader` | relaxed refusal + additive | none for crosstabs that declare columns (byte-identical). Drop a synthetic constant column field built only to get a Total column; stop expecting `PULSE_CROSSTAB_EMPTY_COLUMNS` | U47 (E2-S1, #326 / #328) |
| a crosstab held exactly ONE cell aggregation (`crosstab.cell`, required in the payload schema and the MCP-bound crosstab schema); a `cell` with a `field` and no `type` was not smart-defaulted (its empty type reached the aggregator factory unresolved); `types.CrosstabSpec.Cell` marshalled as `"cell": null` when nil | `crosstab.cells` (`types.CrosstabSpec.Cells []*Aggregation`, mutually exclusive with `cell`) carries several measures: each runs exactly as its own single-cell crosstab and becomes a stub row under every row key (`row_header` gains `{_measure, MEASURE}`, row r measure m at r×M+m, `matrix.cell_labels`), with the per-measure column margins / grand total in `matrix.measures` and `components.crosstab.measures`; only pairwise overlays run on such a host, once per measure (every other overlay, and a Compose overlay reading the slot, is `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE`, reason `multi_measure_host`). An untyped `cell` (or `cells` entry) with a `field` is now smart-defaulted like an aggregation slot (`defaults_applied` path `["Crosstab", "Cell", "Type"]`). `cell` is `omitempty` and no longer `required` (predict refuses neither-set with `PULSE_CROSSTAB_MISSING_CELL`). New: `PULSE_CROSSTAB_CELLS_INVALID` / `_DUPLICATE_LABEL`, feature `capability:crosstab_cells` (hard edge on `capability:crosstab`; both shipped example profiles keep it), `descriptor.CrosstabCapability.SupportsCells` / `CellsRules`, `types.CrosstabMeasureMargins` / `CrosstabMeasureComponents` / `CrosstabMeasureField` / `CrosstabMeasureType`, `MatrixPayload.CellLabels` / `Measures`, `CrosstabComponents.Measures`, `(*CrosstabSpec).HasMeasures` / `CellAggregations` / `MeasureLabels` / `MeasureSpec` / `MeasureRowHeader` / `CellSlotPath` / `CellsFaults` | additive + relaxed refusal | none for single-`cell` requests with a typed cell (byte-identical). A custom feature profile that keeps `capability:crosstab` must add `capability:crosstab_cells` to accept `cells`; replace a Compose of one crosstab per figure with one `cells` crosstab where only pairwise overlays are needed | U47 (E2-S2, #326) |
| the `OVERLAY_PAIRWISE_*` family emitted RAW p-values only; which leg of a pair was higher and whether it was significant (the survey-table significance letter) were left to the embedder | opt-in `params.letters` (`{}` or `{"alpha": a}`, `0 < a < 1`; `types.PairwiseOverlayParams.Letters` / `types.PairwiseLetters`) on every pairwise kind adds `payload.parts.letters`, a MATRIX on the HOST coordinates whose cell, for every pair-axis index compared with another, is a `types.PairwiseLetterCell {letter, group, higher, above}`: the index's letter (A, B, … restarting per comparison group — one group, or one per `pair_along_dim` bucket; the implicit Total column and a lone bucket member get no cell), the letters it is higher than (the kernel's statistic sign) and the significant subset. Significance is `significant_adjusted` when a multiplicity correction ran on the layer, else raw p < 0.05; an explicit `alpha` compares `p_adjusted` (or raw p) instead and is echoed as `summary.parameters.letters_alpha`. An alpha outside (0, 1), an unknown key inside `letters` or a non-object `letters` is `PULSE_OVERLAY_PARAM_MISSING` at predict and runtime. New: `types.PairwiseLetters`, `types.PairwiseLetterCell`, `types.PairwiseLettersRefusal` | additive | none — a spec without `letters` is byte-identical | U47 (E3-S1, #327) |
| a survey banner table (Total \| Gender \| Age \| Region × several KPI stubs) needed one hand-written crosstab slot per section × group, and the embedder stitched the slots side by side and re-lettered them | opt-in `ComposedRequest.banner` (`types.BannerSpec {request, sections, groups, total, overlays}`, gated by `capability:compose_banner`) expands into ordinary crosstab slots labelled `<section>:<block>` (Total first, then the groups) after the explicit and sweep slots, and adds `ComposedResponse.banner` (`types.BannerResult` → `BannerSectionResult` / `BannerBlock` / `BannerLetters`): each section's slot matrices side by side, per-block margins, letters re-assigned banner-wide with the Total block compared to nothing. `MaxComposeSlots` counts the banner slots; a malformed banner is `PULSE_BANNER_INVALID` at predict and runtime; `descriptor.BannerSummary` on the predict result | additive | none — a banner-free request and response are byte-identical | U47 (E3-S3, #326) |
| a wave-over-wave significance test needed the embedder to run its own test between consecutive periods (`OVERLAY_DELTA_VS_PRIOR` / `_INDEX_VS_PRIOR` report the size of the change only) | three inferential SERIES overlay kinds `OVERLAY_T_VS_PRIOR` (Welch t), `OVERLAY_Z_VS_PRIOR` (normal z) and `OVERLAY_PROP_Z_VS_PRIOR` (pooled two-proportion z) test each period against the last one with a value (Ref `Prior`, lag 1) on a grouped Process or a crosstab with no column grouper. Each `SeriesEntry` carries `summary.statistic` + `summary.p_value`; the first period has a `null` statistic and no `p_value` (no multiplicity member). The mean kinds need an `AGG_WELFORD` / `AGG_VARIANCE` / `AGG_STDDEV` measure, the proportion kind an `AGG_AVERAGE` of a 0/1 indicator on an unnormalised host; legs are read off components (disabled → `PULSE_OVERLAY_COMPONENTS_REQUIRED`); weighted hosts read N*; buffered. New: `types.OverlayKindTVsPrior` / `OverlayKindZVsPrior` / `OverlayKindPropZVsPrior` (also in `types.AllOverlayKinds`); both shipped example profiles list them | additive | none — existing requests are byte-identical. A custom feature profile must list the kinds (and their measure dependency) to offer them | U47 (E3-S2, #328) |
| computing survey weights (rim weighting / raking, cell weighting) needed an external tool, and the resulting column had to be imported back before a request could weight by it | `Pulse.Rake(ctx, RakeRequest) (*RakeResult, error)` rakes a cohort to inline margins (`margins[]`: `field` + `targets`, or a joint `fields` + `cells` for cell weighting; `targets_as` shares / counts), optionally per `by` group, from a `base_weight`, under `min_weight` / `max_weight` caps relative to the mean, to `tol` within `max_iter`, normalised to Σw = n or `total`, and writes a NEW single-file cohort: every source column verbatim plus a nullable `f64` weight (`weight_field`, default `rake_weight`; `overwrite` replaces an existing column in place). `RakeResult` reports `iterations` / `converged` / `residual`, per-margin (and per-group) `observed` / `target` / `achieved` shares and `efficiency {n, sum_weights, n_eff, deff_kish, efficiency_pct, weight_min, weight_max, weight_range_ratio, weight_cv, n_capped}` — undefined figures `null`; `deff_kish` is the Kish unequal-weighting design effect only. The output is a PROBABILITY weight. New: `pulse.RakeRequest` / `RakeMargin` / `RakeCell` / `RakeResult` / `RakeMarginReport` / `RakeCategoryReport` / `RakeGroupReport` / `RakeEfficiency`, `RakeDefaultMaxIter` / `RakeDefaultTol` / `RakeDefaultWeightField`, codes `PULSE_RAKE_INVALID` / `_UNKNOWN_CATEGORY` / `_UNTARGETED_CATEGORY` / `_MISSING_VALUE` / `_EMPTY_CELL` (refusals) and `_NOT_CONVERGED` / `_SIDECARS_INVALIDATED` (warnings), `observe.OpRake` (`rake`; one more `pulse_operations_total` label value), feature `capability:rake` (the shipped `survey-crosstab` profile lists it). The `Warn` log record now also fires for a `Materialize` result's warnings (previously skipped). The CLI leaf `pulse rake` (`--margins` / `--request` as a JSON literal or file, one flag per scalar key) and the MCP tool `pulse_rake` (the request spread at the root), both gated by `capability:rake`, take the same request | additive | none — nothing existing changes. An adapter that pre-resolves per-kind instruments from `observe.AllOperationKinds()` picks up `rake` automatically | U47 (E4-S1, E4-S2, #329) |
| `pulse export spss --labels` / an `ExportJob` with `Labels` / `LabelResolver` and a `.sav` target was refused `PULSE_SPSS_EXPORT_UNSUPPORTED`; a native cohort's categoricals always exported as STRING variables with no value labels | the binding is written as `.sav` VALUE LABELS: a bound categorical becomes a NUMERIC variable — codes are the dictionary's own integers when every entry is one, else the table's entry order, else sorted dictionary order (see the E4-S7 row below) — labelled from the table (an entry the table misses keeps its text). `replace` and `augment` behave alike (no cell rewritten). New `io.SPSSWriterOptions.ValueLabels` (`[]io.SPSSValueLabels{Field, Labels}`, nil labels = the entry text) and `io.CohortSource.ValueLabels` (the binding resolved per dictionary entry by `ExportJob.Run` / `Predict`). Refusals: beside an honoured SPSS sidecar `PULSE_SPSS_EXPORT_UNSUPPORTED` (`--ignore-sidecar` to relabel), `PULSE_LABEL_FIELD_UNKNOWN` / `_NOT_CATEGORICAL` / `_DUPLICATE_BINDING`, predicted alike. Unbound exports are byte-identical | Behaviour change (refusal → output) + additive | Drop any workaround that relabelled into a narrowed cohort before `export spss`. A custom `io.CohortWriter` keeps refusing on `Labelled`; read `ValueLabels` only if the format has a native value-label slot | U47 |
| a labelled `.sav` export (`--labels`, `ExportJob.Labels`, `io.SPSSWriterOptions.ValueLabels`) coded a bound categorical whose entries are not all integers by dictionary POSITION + 1 — first-seen order, so the code a wave or region got depended on which row came first (wave 1 = "2025 Q3", region 1 = "South"); value labels went out in dictionary order | with an entry order — new optional `pulse.LabelTable.Order` (validated at `New`: no repeated entry, Rows keys only, else `PULSE_EXTENSION_PARAM_INVALID`), filled from the file's key order for a `PULSE_LABEL_TABLES_DIR` table, or `io.SPSSValueLabels.Order` — the code is the entry's TABLE position + 1 (stable across every cohort bound to the table; unlisted entries follow, byte-wise sorted); with none, rank + 1 in byte-wise sorted dictionary order. Value labels go out in code order. Integer dictionaries keep their own codes. New `io.CohortSource.ValueLabelOrder` carries the order to a cohort writer (a resolver reports it through an optional `Order(field string) []string` method). Unbound exports are byte-identical | Behaviour break (labelled `.sav` exports only) + additive | Re-run a labelled export whose SPSS syntax addresses codes; list a programmatic `Rows` table's keys in `Order` to pin its codes, or rely on sorted order | U47 |
| a synthesised `.sav` (a native cohort, or `--ignore-sidecar`) wrote each `set_*` indicator variable with NO variable label, so re-importing the `.sav` drew one `PULSE_FIELD_DESCRIPTION_LOW_QUALITY` warning per indicator | every indicator carries the variable label `<set description or name>: <option>`, held to 255 bytes (the set half shortened first, cut on a rune boundary); names, codes and the response-set definition are unchanged | Behaviour change (`.sav` bytes of a synthesised export with a `set_*` field) | Nothing to adapt; a pinned digest of such a `.sav` moves | U47 |
| `pulse export predict` had no `--labels` flag, so the `.sav` value-label refusals were predicted only through `io.ExportJob.Predict` with a hand-built resolver; on the CLI a field bound twice in `--labels` was an UNCODED error (envelope placeholder `CLI_INPUT`) on every leaf | `pulse export predict --labels field=table[:mode]` (repeatable) and `Pulse.PredictExport(ctx, *io.ExportJob) (*io.PredictReport, error)` — `ExportJob.Predict` with the resolver built from the instance's label tables as `Export` builds it, reported as a `predict` operation — refuse `PULSE_LABEL_TABLE_UNKNOWN`, `PULSE_LABEL_FIELD_UNKNOWN`, `_NOT_CATEGORICAL`, `PULSE_SPSS_EXPORT_UNSUPPORTED` (beside a sidecar) with the export's code; a CLI field bound twice is `PULSE_LABEL_DUPLICATE_BINDING` on every leaf | Additive + error-code change (CLI duplicate binding) | Call `PredictExport` instead of `ExportJob.Predict` when the job carries `Labels`; match `PULSE_LABEL_DUPLICATE_BINDING` rather than the message text | U47 |
| `Pulse.Materialize` / `Pulse.Rake` (and `pulse api materialize` / `pulse rake` / `pulse_materialize` / `pulse_rake`) wrote no sidecar beside the output, so a derived cohort of an SPSS-origin cohort exported with no value labels, codes or measure levels | the source's SPSS metadata sidecar (`cohort.pulse.spss.json`) is CARRIED: rewritten for the new schema (a plain numeric variable per added column; a rake-replaced variable goes plain numeric) and re-fingerprinted over the output. A stale / invalid source sidecar, or a replaced variable a response set / the derived registry names, is not carried and adds ONE warning with the cause's code (`PULSE_SPSS_SIDECAR_STALE` / `_INVALID`) to the result's `warnings`; a carried sidecar is never in `invalidated_sidecars` | Behaviour change (a new file beside the output; a possible new warning) | Expect `<out>.spss.json` beside a derived cohort of an SPSS-origin source; a caller asserting an empty `warnings` must allow the carry warning on a stale source | U47 |
| `AGG_WELFORD` on a field the cohort schema does not carry — an attribute output (an `ATTR_FORMULA`, `ATTR_ZSCORE`, … `label`) — predicted clean but the runtime refused it `PROCESSING_CONFIG` "AGG_WELFORD: unknown field <label>", on every host (aggregation, grouped, crosstab cell, Compose slot), so the Welford-reading overlays (`OVERLAY_T_VS_PRIOR` / `_Z_VS_PRIOR`, `OVERLAY_PAIRWISE_WELCH_T` / `_TWO_MEANS_Z`, `OVERLAY_T_VS_REF` / `_Z_VS_REF`) could not score a derived measure | `AGG_WELFORD` reads an attribute output like `AGG_VARIANCE` / `AGG_STDDEV` do: the triple equals the one over a stored field holding the same values. A name neither the schema nor an attribute defines stays refused by the shared field-reference rule, predicted alike | relaxed refusal | none — drop any workaround that materialised the derived column first | U47 (E4-S6) |
| `OVERLAY_PROP_Z_VS_PRIOR` scoring an `AGG_WELFORD` aggregation on a grouped Process was refused by the shared scored-value rule: `PROCESSING_CONFIG`, `details.reason: "non_scalar"` | refused by the kind's own measure rule, as every other non-`AGG_AVERAGE` built-in measure is: `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE` with `details {kind, measure_type: "AGG_WELFORD"}`, at predict and runtime alike (the crosstab host already did) | refusal code change | a caller matching the old code or `reason` matches `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE` / `measure_type`; score an `AGG_AVERAGE` of the 0/1 indicator | U47 (E4-S6) |
| `OVERLAY_PAIRWISE_PROP_Z` / `OVERLAY_PAIRWISE_PROBIT_T` with `params.p_source` omitted on an UNNORMALISED crosstab (`normalize` absent or `none`) read every cell as a 0..100 percentage (`cell_value_pct`, ÷100) whatever the cell was — over a mean cell (`AGG_AVERAGE` of a 0/1 indicator, 0.86 read as 0.0086; `AGG_WELFORD`, `AGG_MEDIAN`, …) every pair was scored on a meaningless proportion, silently (the brand-tracker 86% vs 63% at p = 0.87) | that request is refused `PULSE_OVERLAY_PARAM_MISSING` (`details {kind, param: "p_source", cell_type}`) at predict and runtime alike, per measure on a multi-measure host, whenever the cell is a BUILT-IN aggregation other than a count or sum (`AGG_COUNT`, `AGG_SUM`, `AGG_NULL_COUNT`, `AGG_DISTINCT_COUNT`, `AGG_DISTINCT_SUM`, `AGG_MODE_COUNT`, `AGG_FREQUENCY`, `AGG_SET_CARDINALITY_SUM`); an explicit `p_source`, a normalised host, a count / sum cell and an extension cell are unchanged | new refusal | set `p_source: "cell_value"` when the mean cell is a 0..1 proportion (an `AGG_AVERAGE` of a 0/1 indicator), or `"cell_value_pct"` to keep the old reading of a 0..100 cell (an `AGG_AVERAGE` of a 0/100 indicator) byte-identical | U47 (E4-S6) |
| a `ComposedRequest.banner` copied the base request's `labels` onto EVERY banner slot, so a binding on a group's column field (`region`) warned `PULSE_LABEL_NOT_APPLIED` on the Total slot and every other group's slot; a banner section could not carry its own overlays — every group slot got the banner-level `overlays` | each banner slot keeps only the base bindings its crosstab rows or columns group by (a binding no banner slot groups by stays on every slot and still warns there; the slot's echoed `labels` — and a warning's `details.index` — are the slot's own list), so the stitched banner carries display labels with no spurious warning. New optional `types.BannerSection.Overlays`: when non-empty they REPLACE the banner-level `overlays` on that section's group slots (the base request's overlays still lead every slot, the Total slot receives neither), and that section's `BannerLetters` carry the new `Section: true` (`section`, `omitempty`) with `Overlay` indexing the section's list | behaviour change (fewer warnings; slot `labels` echo) + additive | none for banners without base `labels` or section `overlays` (byte-identical); a caller counting the per-slot `PULSE_LABEL_NOT_APPLIED` warnings of a labelled banner sees only the ones for bindings no slot groups by | U47 (E4-S8) |

## Changes from U48 (experiment readout)

Ships in the next alpha. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; every additive row is byte-identical for a request that does not use it.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the sample-ratio check took a detour — a 1:1 design cohort and a `FacetSchema` `OVERLAY_CHISQ_VS_POP` run, or a hand-computed z — because `TEST_CHISQ` required both `rows` and `cols` and `TEST_PROP_Z` required `split_by` | `TEST_CHISQ` optional `params.expected` (`{level: proportion}`, each > 0, ≥ 2 labels, summing to 1 ± 1e-9, never renormalised; `cols` omitted) runs a goodness-of-fit χ² of `rows` (`Variant` `goodness_of_fit`, df = labels − 1; `details` `labels` sorted, `observed`, `expected`, `expected_proportions`, `n`, `expected_min`) — the very statistic and p of `OVERLAY_CHISQ_VS_POP` against the design. `TEST_PROP_Z` optional `params.p0` in (0, 1) with `split_by` absent runs the one-proportion z (`Variant` `one_proportion`; z² equals the two-level goodness-of-fit χ²). New codes `PULSE_TEST_EXPECTED_INVALID` (bad map, beside `cols`, a packed_bool label ≠ `"0"` / `"1"`) and `PULSE_TEST_P0_INVALID` (out of range, or beside `split_by` — refused, not ignored like `TEST_T` `mu`), predict too; `PULSE_TEST_EXPECTED_LABEL_MISSING` (an observed level `expected` omits) runtime only. A listed level never observed counts as 0. Absent = byte-identical | additive | replace the facet detour with `{"type": "TEST_CHISQ", "rows": "<arm>", "params": {"expected": {"control": 0.5, "treatment": 0.5}}}` | U48 (E1-S1) |
| `TEST_CHISQ` (independence form) reported `details.row_labels` / `col_labels` in FIRST-SEEN order — the order the rows / cols levels first appeared in the cohort — and indexed `details.contingency`, `row_totals` / `col_totals` (Σw when weighted) the same way; every other test sorted its labels | both axes sorted byte-wise (`sort.Strings`), as every other test and the goodness-of-fit form do; `contingency` and the margins are indexed by the sorted labels. χ², df, p, `expected_min`, Cramér's V and φ are unchanged (order-invariant) | behaviour change (output order) | read cells through `row_labels` / `col_labels`, never by assumed position | U48 (E1-S2) |
| `TEST_FISHER_EXACT` built its 2×2 table `[[a, b], [c, d]]` and `row_labels` / `col_labels` in first-seen order, so `statistic` / `details.odds_ratio` (ad/bc) came out inverted (1/OR) whenever the second level of an axis appeared first | rows and columns sorted byte-wise; the odds ratio is that of the first SORTED row and column. The two-sided p is unchanged | behaviour change (moves the odds ratio) | read `row_labels` / `col_labels` to orient the ratio; invert it if your reference level sorts second | U48 (E1-S2) |
| the `PULSE_TEST_TIES_DOMINATE` warning (every rank test: `TEST_MANN_WHITNEY_U`, `TEST_WILCOXON_SR`, `TEST_KRUSKAL_WALLIS`, `TEST_SPEARMAN_R`, `TEST_KENDALL_TAU` and their post variants) said only "≥ 50% of … are tied; asymptotic p-value is unreliable"; the code's metadata the same | the text adds that the test then mostly compares the share of tied values, not their order (one shared helper); `codeMetadata` `Message` and `Hint` say the same and point at comparing the tied shares directly. The code, its trigger and every number are unchanged | behaviour change (warning text) | match on the `PULSE_TEST_TIES_DOMINATE` code prefix, never the prose | U48 (E1-S2) |
| predict refused a `TEST_PROP_Z` with no `field`, or no `split_by` (and no `params.p0`), `SERVICE_VALIDATION` while the runtime refused the same request `PROCESSING_CONFIG` | predict raises `PROCESSING_CONFIG`, the runtime's code and message (and every sibling test's runtime missing-`split_by` code) | behaviour change (predict error code) | branch on `PROCESSING_CONFIG` for these two refusals | U48 (E1-S2) |
| `TEST_PROP_Z` on a categorical outcome never checked `params.success` against the outcome's dictionary, so a misspelt level (`"Yes"` for `"yes"`, `"true"` on a `"0"` / `"1"` categorical) ran and reported zero successes — a confident 0% rate, or a `PULSE_TEST_VARIANCE_ZERO` refusal — in both the two-group and the one-proportion (`p0`) form | refused `PULSE_TEST_SUCCESS_VALUE_MISSING` ("params.success \"…\" is not a level of categorical_… field …"; `details.field` / `field_type` / `success`) by predict and the runtime alike, the packed_bool rule's code (one shared check, `internal/fieldaccept.SuccessLevelRefusal`). Over a shard archive the canonical union dictionary is the judge, so a level only a later shard holds is accepted | behaviour change (newly refused) | spell `success` exactly as `pulse inspect` lists the dictionary entry | U48 (E1-S2) |
| two-group tests (`TEST_T` two-sample, `TEST_WELCH`, `TEST_Z_TWO_SAMPLE`, `TEST_PROP_Z`, `TEST_MANN_WHITNEY_U`) always read their groups alphabetically, so a readout's lift came out control − variant; `TEST_WELCH` / `TEST_Z_TWO_SAMPLE` / `TEST_MANN_WHITNEY_U` accepted no params | optional `params.reference` (a `split_by` level; `"0"` / `"1"` on a packed_bool) is read second: `diff`, its interval, the statistic, `cohens_d` / `cohens_h`, Mann-Whitney `u_a` / `u_b` / `r_a` / `r_b` / `z` / `rank_biserial` read other − reference, every per-group array (`sum_weights` / `n_eff` included) follows, `details.reference` echoes it. New codes `PULSE_TEST_REFERENCE_INVALID` (not a level, or no `split_by`; predict too) and `PULSE_TEST_REFERENCE_MISSING` (neither observed group; runtime only, a null result per group when grouped). Absent = alphabetical, byte-identical | additive | set `reference` to the control arm and drop the sign flip | U48 (E1-S3) |
| predict refused a generic test with no `field`, a paired / bivariate test with no `field2`, and `TEST_ANOVA_RM` with no `subject_field` `SERVICE_VALIDATION` (messages "requires field2 (second numeric column)", "requires subject_field") and passed one with no `split_by` (e.g. `TEST_MANN_WHITNEY_U`), while the runtime refused each `PROCESSING_CONFIG`; post-tests the same | predict raises `PROCESSING_CONFIG` with the runtime constructor's message for every missing required slot, tier 1 and post-tests alike (one shared table, `internal/fieldaccept.RequiredSlotsRefusal`). An extension test with no `field` keeps `SERVICE_VALIDATION` | behaviour change (predict error code) | branch on `PROCESSING_CONFIG` for a missing `field` / `field2` / `split_by` / `subject_field` | U48 (E1-S3) |
| `TEST_WELCH` with no `split_by` silently ran the one-sample t-test (reported as `TEST_T`, variant `one_sample`); its missing-`field` message named `TEST_T` | refused `PROCESSING_CONFIG` "TEST_WELCH requires split_by" by predict and the runtime; a missing `field` says "TEST_WELCH requires field" | behaviour change (newly refused) | use `TEST_T` for a one-sample test | U48 (E1-S3) |
| regression coefficient p-values joined no `multiplicity` family (`regressions[].multiplicity` was an unknown field) | `RegressionSpec.multiplicity` (`types.RegressionMultiplicity`: `method`, `family` — `request` default or `compose` —, `alpha`, `coefficients`) opts a REG_OLS / REG_GLM fit's coefficient p-values into a family; `RegressionResult` gains `p_adjusted` / `significant_adjusted` maps and a `multiplicity` echo beside the untouched `p_values`. Only a regression's OWN block opts it in, so an existing request-level or `Options.DefaultMultiplicity` block corrects exactly what it did before. A non-`none` method on `REG_BAYES_LINEAR` and an unknown or repeated coefficient are refused `PULSE_MULTIPLICITY_INVALID` (predict too) | additive | add `{"multiplicity": {"coefficients": ["<predictor>"]}}` to a regression to correct it with the request's tests | U48 (E2-S1) |
| predict's `p_values {total, uncorrected, …}` ignored regressions, so a regression-only request reported none and `PULSE_ADVISORY_MANY_TESTS` never counted them | a REG_OLS / REG_GLM regression counts one p-value per non-intercept predictor (uncorrected unless its own block claims them; REG_BAYES_LINEAR none), so `PredictResult.PValues` appears on regression requests and the advisory can fire on ≥ 10 uncorrected coefficients | behaviour change (predict output) | expect `p_values` on predict for a request with regressions; add a regression `multiplicity` block to clear the advisory | U48 (E2-S1) |
| covariate adjustment (CUPED) took two round trips — a `REG_OLS` slope and the covariate mean pasted into an `ATTR_FORMULA`, then a test on the derived column (or one long `total()` formula) | optional `params.covariate` (a numeric pre-period column) on `TEST_T` two-sample, `TEST_WELCH` and `TEST_Z_TWO_SAMPLE`, one pass from per-group moments (streams): `diff`, its interval, the statistic, p, `mean` / `variance` and `cohens_d` are the adjusted figures (equal to the two-step); `details` add `diff_unadjusted`, `theta`, `covariate_mean`, `variance_reduction` (`null` with `theta` when the covariate is constant) and `n_covariate_dropped` (null-covariate rows, dropped listwise). Composes with `reference`. New code `PULSE_TEST_COVARIATE_INVALID` (predict too). Absent = byte-identical | additive | replace the two-step with `"params": {"covariate": "<pre-period column>"}` | U48 (E2-S2) |
| `params.covariate` on any other test (or a post-test) was refused `PULSE_REQUEST_UNKNOWN_FIELD` like any unknown params key | refused `PULSE_TEST_COVARIATE_INVALID` (`details.reason` `unsupported_test`, `supported_tests`), predict and every runtime funnel alike | behaviour change (error code) | branch on `PULSE_TEST_COVARIATE_INVALID`; only the two-sample mean tests take a covariate | U48 (E2-S2) |
| `params.covariate` on `TEST_PROP_Z` was refused `PULSE_TEST_COVARIATE_INVALID` (`unsupported_test`) | accepted on the two-proportion form: the success indicator is adjusted (Variant `two_proportion_adjusted`, z = adjusted `diff` / its Wald SE; `proportion`, `diff`, the interval and `cohens_h` adjusted; the same `details` keys as the mean tests). With `p0` it is refused (`without_split_by`). Every covariate test now runs under both weight kinds as the weighted two-step (θ, x̄ on the raw weights) and per partition under `groups` | additive | adjust a conversion test with `"params": {"success": "yes", "covariate": "<pre-period column>"}` | U48 (E2-S3) |
| a request with no aggregation whose work was tier-1 tests or regressions (no matrix) ran on the BUFFERED arm — every record materialised — and predict said `streamable: false` (`streamable_reasons` "no aggregations: streaming path requires at least one OnlineAggregator"); authors added a throwaway `AGG_COUNT` to stream | it runs on the STREAMING arm whenever every other gate admits it (streamable tests only — rank tests, `TEST_KS`, `TEST_FISHER_EXACT` … still buffer —, a streamable regression, no grouper / feature / two-pass attribute), and predict says `streamable: true` with no reason (parity-gated). The `Response` is byte-identical to the buffered run, a regression's `multiplicity` fold included. Streaming chunk shape, stated precisely: such a request has no `data` row on either arm, so `ProcessStream` yields no row and `ProcessStreamResult` sends NO chunk — only the `StreamCompleted` terminator with `TotalRows` 0 — exactly as before; the tests / regressions are read from `Process`'s `Response.Tests` / `Regressions`, never from a chunk. What moves is the arm: `observe.OperationResult.Arm` (and the arm metric label) reads `streaming`, not `buffered`, and resident memory no longer scales with the record count. A request with no aggregation, matrix, test or regression (empty; filters, attributes or features only) still buffers; its reason now reads "no aggregations, matrices, tests or regressions: streaming path requires per-record work" | behaviour change (execution arm, predict output) | drop the throwaway `AGG_COUNT`; branch on `streamable`, never on the reason prose | U48 (E3-S1) |
| one Process request carrying tier-1 tests AND a regression (the whole A/B readout: SRM check, lifts, regression adjustment) ran on the BUFFERED arm even when each half alone streamed, and predict said `streamable: false` (`streamable_reasons` "regression with tier-1 tests runs via the buffered path"); a streaming readout had to split into a Compose batch | it runs on the STREAMING arm whenever each half would stream on its own (streamable tests, a streamable regression, no grouper / feature / two-pass attribute), and predict says `streamable: true` with no reason (parity-gated). The ungrouped scan folds every filter-passing record into the tests and the regression in the buffered arm's record order, so `Response.Tests` / `Regressions` / `Warnings` are byte-identical to the buffered run — weighted or not, a `request`-family `multiplicity` spanning both included. The reason string is gone; a grouped request, a robust covariance, a rank test or a two-pass attribute still buffers with its own reason. `observe.OperationResult.Arm` reads `streaming`, and the memory estimate follows that arm | behaviour change (execution arm, predict output) | run the readout as one request (or keep the Compose batch — same figures); branch on `streamable`, never on the reason prose | U48 (E3-S3) |
| predict's `sizes` gave any ungrouped request with no aggregation and no window a `data` section of one row per record (`upper_bound`; ~19 MB for a two-test request on 40,000 rows), and `MaxEstimatedMemory` costed a tests- or regression-only request on the buffered arm | such a request emits no `data` row (the runtime omits the key), so `sizes` carries no `data` section — tests, regressions, attributes, features or an empty request alike; a windows-only request still sizes one row per record. The memory estimate follows the streaming arm, so a tests- or regression-only request a `MaxEstimatedMemory` limit used to refuse (`PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`) may now be admitted | behaviour change (predict output, limit verdict) | read the `tests` / `regressions` sections for these requests; re-check any `MaxEstimatedMemory` headroom tuned around the old figure | U48 (E3-S1) |
| a ProcessChain stage carrying tests or regressions and no aggregation was refused `PULSE_CHAIN_NOT_MERGEABLE` "chain stage is not mergeable: it has no aggregator" | the same code, message "chain stage is not mergeable: windows, features, tests, post-tests and regressions are excluded" (the shared merge gate now names the slot that excludes it). The verdict is unchanged: tests and regressions never merge | behaviour change (error message) | match on the code, never the prose | U48 (E3-S1) |
| predict-compose (`PredictCompose`, `pulse api predict-compose`, `pulse_predict` `composed`) said nothing per slot: a readout whose slots each corrected their own p-values (`family: "request"`) looked fully corrected, with no hint that no correction spanned the batch | `ComposePredictResult.Slots` (`[]descriptor.ComposeSlotPredict`: `request`, `label`, `streamable` + `streamable_reasons` — Process predict's verdict for the slot —, `p_values`, `families` `[]descriptor.PValueFamily {id, family, method, count, basis}`) on every batch whose slots are judged; new advisory `PULSE_ADVISORY_COMPOSE_FAMILIES_SPLIT` when ≥ 2 slots emit p-values into ≥ 2 distinct non-`compose` families with ≥ 10 p-values in total (`details.suggested` `{"multiplicity": {"method": M, "family": "compose"}}`). Never a warning; `SuppressAdvisories` accepts the code | additive (predict output: the `slots` key appears on every judged compose predict) | read `slots[i].families[].id` to see which p-values are corrected together; set `family: "compose"` on each slot's block to pool a readout, or suppress the code | U48 (E3-S2) |

## Changes from U49 (model workbench)

Ships in the next alpha. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`.

Release-notes grouping (rows below; there is no separate behaviour-breaks file — this section is the alpha's release-note source). **Behaviour breaks that move numbers or outputs** — `REG_OLS` `l1` / `elasticnet` now penalize raw-unit coefficients (glmnet `standardize = FALSE`, one `alpha` meaning family-wide; `l2` unchanged), and a coefficient the penalty zeroes carries no p-value (it was `p = 0`); `GROUP_QUANTILE` / `GROUP_RANGE` / `GROUP_ROUNDED` rows come out in bucket order (a `sort` on the key column now scrambles them); a failed regression / test / matrix slot or Compose fit is **partial success by default** (`types.SlotError`, `PULSE_SLOT_FAILED`, `PULSE_FIT_UNAVAILABLE`; `Options.Strict` keeps fail-fast); the leave-one-out jackknife deletes fitted rows only (its SEs grow on data with dropped rows); `REG_GLM` last-digit drift (per-row start, unclamped variance). **Refusal changes** — every window slot refusal is `PULSE_WINDOW_INVALID` on predict and run alike, and the run now refuses slots it used to execute (a `field` on row number / rank / dense rank, a missing `order_by` or required `frame`, …); `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_SEPARATION` replaces `NO_CONVERGE` / `RANK_DEFICIENT` for a separated fit; a non-bool expression filter is `PULSE_FORMULA_COMPILE_ERROR` at compile; `PULSE_RANGE_TABLE_KIND_MISMATCH`, and predict reports a date-range operator's unknown table; predict raises the penalty-knob refusals. **Relaxed / newly runs** — a weighted jackknife (JK1) runs where it was refused; strong-but-unseparated GLM fits converge; a streaming `FEAT_TARGET_ENCODE` / quantile `FEAT_BUCKETIZE` over an earlier feature's column computes values instead of nulls (bug fix); `Explain` on a Compose request agrees with `PredictCompose` (its fits bound), names a selection regression by the predictors it kept and reads a Compose result's regression fits. **Wire changes** — `RangeTableInfo.ranges` `omitempty` plus `kind` / `numeric_ranges`; manifest `forbids_field` on the order-only windows (their `accepts_types` is `[]`). **Additive** — `AGG_AUC`, `WIN_SHARE`, `WIN_LIFT`, `GROUP_NUMERIC_RANGES` + numeric range tables, penalised `REG_GLM`, `jackknife_groups` / `jackknife_by` + `PULSE_ADVISORY_JACKKNIFE_COST` (Process and Compose predict), `FEAT_TARGET_ENCODE` `fit_on` / `folds` / `rng_seed`.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
